Does that sound like what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself having repetitive thoughts that are difficult to control?\n\nPatient: Yes… I guess I do, though I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. It’s more like I’ll tell myself, “Daniela, enough, focus on this report,” and then one small thought turns into five more. Before I know it, I’ve spent half an hour checking messages or replaying what could go wrong. I know that probably sounds a little silly, but it’s hard to shut it off sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any physical symptoms like tremors, sweating, or rapid heartbeat when you're feeling wound up or anxious?\n\nPatient: Not really, no… I mean, I haven’t noticed shaking or sweating or my heart racing in a big way. I might feel wound up, but I don’t think it shows up physically like that. At least, not that I’ve paid attention to.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Work gets done, and I still show up for my family and church. But I do put off reports sometimes because I know I’ll end up checking them over and over, and then they take longer than they should. And I guess I’ve been a little quicker to sound short with people I care about, which I feel bad about and I’ve apologized for. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are your worries usually about a specific situation or topic, or are they more general and varied throughout the day?\n\nPatient: It’s more general, I think. I wish it were just one thing, but my mind kind of moves from one concern to another during the day. Like I’ll worry about my sister in Arecibo, then whether I missed something at the office, then my husband driving home, or church commitments, bills… things like that.\n\nAnd I know some of it is me getting ahead of myself, preparing for problems that haven’t even happened. I don’t mean to make it sound dramatic, but it does jump around like that. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Is there one particular thing that you worry about more than others?\n\nPatient: Not one single thing, no… or at least I don’t think so. It’s more like my mind keeps moving from one concern to the next. I’ll think about my sister in Arecibo, then whether I missed something at the office, then my husband driving home, church commitments, bills… it sort of rotates.\n\nI try to tell myself I’m just being prepared, but sometimes I’m preparing for problems that haven’t even happened. Does that make sense?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but my mind keeps jumping from one thing to the next. I’ll worry about my sister in Arecibo, then whether I missed something at the office, then my husband driving home, or church things I said I’d help with, bills… little things, mostly. But I catch myself kind of preparing for problems that haven’t even happened. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any episodes where you felt an intense fear or discomfort that reached a peak within minutes?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like a sudden wave that peaks quickly. I get nervous and tense, but I wouldn’t say I’ve had an episode like that. At least, not that I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding any situations or activities because of your worries or tension?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’ve been avoiding things, no… at least not in a major way. I still go to work and church and do the things I’m supposed to do. Sometimes I might hesitate or put something off a little if I’m feeling tense, but I wouldn’t say I’m really avoiding situations. Does that sound like what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself having repetitive thoughts that are difficult to control?\n\nPatient: Yes… I guess I do, though I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. I know that probably sounds a little silly, but it’s hard to shut it off sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any physical symptoms like tremors, sweating, or rapid heartbeat when you're feeling wound up or anxious?\n\nPatient: Not really, no… I mean, I haven’t noticed shaking or sweating or my heart racing in a big way. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but my mind keeps jumping from one thing to the next. But I catch myself kind of preparing for problems that haven’t even happened. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any episodes where you felt an intense fear or discomfort that reached a peak within minutes?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like a sudden wave that peaks quickly. I get nervous and tense, but I wouldn’t say I’ve had an episode like that. At least, not that I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding any situations or activities because of your worries or tension?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’ve been avoiding things, no… at least not in a major way. I still go to work and church and do the things I’m supposed to do. Sometimes I might hesitate or put something off a little if I’m feeling tense, but I wouldn’t say I’m really avoiding situations. Does that sound like what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself having repetitive thoughts that are difficult to control?\n\nPatient: Yes… I guess I do, though I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. It’s more like I’ll tell myself, “Daniela, enough, focus on this report,” and then one small thought turns into five more. Before I know it, I’ve spent half an hour checking messages or replaying what could go wrong. I know that probably sounds a little silly, but it’s hard to shut it off sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any physical symptoms like tremors, sweating, or rapid heartbeat when you're feeling wound up or anxious?\n\nPatient: Not really, no… I mean, I haven’t noticed shaking or sweating or my heart racing in a big way. At least, not that I’ve paid attention to.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any traumatic events in your life?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I mean, nothing that I would call traumatic, at least. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n19\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF41.1, F41.0\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I’m not sure if it’s serious enough, but… I’ve been feeling really wound up most of the day. Like my body is sort of bracing for something, even when I’m just sitting at my computer or trying to relax after dinner. It’s uncomfortable, and I thought maybe I should ask about it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or nervous lately?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but my mind keeps jumping from one thing to the next. I’ll worry about my sister in Arecibo, then whether I missed something at the office, then my husband driving home, or church things I said I’d help with, bills… little things, mostly. But I catch myself kind of preparing for problems that haven’t even happened. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any episodes where you felt an intense fear or discomfort that reached a peak within minutes?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like a sudden wave that peaks quickly. I get nervous and tense, but I wouldn’t say I’ve had an episode like that. At least, not that I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding any situations or activities because of your worries or tension?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’ve been avoiding things, no… at least not in a major way. I still go to work and church and do the things I’m supposed to do. Sometimes I might hesitate or put something off a little if I’m feeling tense, but I wouldn’t say I’m really avoiding situations. Does that sound like what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself having repetitive thoughts that are difficult to control?\n\nPatient: Yes… I guess I do, though I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. It’s more like I’ll tell myself, “Daniela, enough, focus on this report,” and then one small thought turns into five more. Before I know it, I’ve spent half an hour checking messages or replaying what could go wrong. I know that probably sounds a little silly, but it’s hard to shut it off sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any physical symptoms like tremors, sweating, or rapid heartbeat when you're feeling wound up or anxious?\n\nPatient: Not really, no… I mean, I haven’t noticed shaking or sweating or my heart racing in a big way. I might feel wound up, but I don’t think it shows up physically like that. At least, not that I’ve paid attention to.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any traumatic events in your life?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I mean, nothing that I would call traumatic, at least. I’ve had stressful times like anyone, but not an event that stands out that way. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way, with the tension and worries?\n\nPatient: I’d say… about a year or so. Maybe thirteen months? It kind of started when my sister in Arecibo began needing a lot more help from the family. I don’t know if that’s too vague, but that’s when I noticed the worrying and tension becoming more regular.\n\nDoctor: Has your worrying or tension been affecting your relationships with family or friends, or your performance at work?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… though I’m still managing, I think. I’m getting my work done, and I still show up for my family and for church and things like that. It’s just… reports take me longer than they used to, because I keep checking them over. And I have caught myself sounding short with people I care about, which I don’t like. I’ve had to apologize a few times. Is that the kind of effect you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your daily routines or hobbies due to your worrying or tension?\n\nPatient: A little, I guess. I’m still doing my normal things — work, helping family, going to church — so it’s not like I’ve stopped everything. But things take me longer than they used to, especially reports, because I keep checking them over. And I’ve noticed I can be a bit short with people I care about, which I feel bad about, so I’ve apologized a few times. I hope that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Are there any small tasks or daily activities that you've started to avoid or put off because they make you feel anxious or worried?\n\nPatient: Not really avoiding, exactly… I mean, I still do what I need to do. Work gets done, and I still show up for my family and church. But I do put off reports sometimes because I know I’ll end up checking them over and over, and then they take longer than they should. And I guess I’ve been a little quicker to sound short with people I care about, which I feel bad about and I’ve apologized for. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are your worries usually about a specific situation or topic, or are they more general and varied throughout the day?\n\nPatient: It’s more general, I think. I wish it were just one thing, but my mind kind of moves from one concern to another during the day. Like I’ll worry about my sister in Arecibo, then whether I missed something at the office, then my husband driving home, or church commitments, bills… things like that.\n\nAnd I know some of it is me getting ahead of myself, preparing for problems that haven’t even happened. I don’t mean to make it sound dramatic, but it does jump around like that. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Is there one particular thing that you worry about more than others?\n\nPatient: Not one single thing, no… or at least I don’t think so. It’s more like my mind keeps moving from one concern to the next. I’ll think about my sister in Arecibo, then whether I missed something at the office, then my husband driving home, church commitments, bills… it sort of rotates.\n\nI try to tell myself I’m just being prepared, but sometimes I’m preparing for problems that haven’t even happened. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a sudden feeling of intense fear or discomfort that reached a peak within minutes, even if it wasn't recently?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like a sudden wave that peaks really fast like that. I get worried and tense, yes, but it’s more of a buildup or it stays in the background. I can’t remember a time where it came on out of nowhere and hit a peak within minutes. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about your anxiety interfering with your overall well-being or quality of life?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… though I don’t want to make it sound like I’m falling apart or anything. I’m still getting my work done, and I show up for my family and church things. But I have noticed it takes me longer to finish reports because I keep double-checking them, more than I used to.\n\nAnd… I guess I’ve been a little short with people I care about sometimes, and then I feel bad and apologize. So yes, it does affect my quality of life some, even if I’m trying to manage it. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Is your worrying more focused on one area of your life, such as work or family, or is it spread across many different areas?\n\nPatient: It’s spread out, I think. I mean, work is part of it, and family too, but it doesn’t stay in one lane. My mind kind of jumps around—from my sister in Arecibo, to whether I missed something at the office, to my husband driving home, church things, bills… little things and bigger things.\n\nI try to frame it as being responsible or prepared, but sometimes I’m preparing for problems that haven’t even happened yet. I don’t know if that answers it the right way, but it’s not just one area.\n\nDoctor: Does your worrying or anxiety feel like it's always there in the background, even when you're not thinking about anything in particular?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s a fair way to put it. I don’t mean that I’m panicking all day or anything like that, but it does feel like I’m sort of wound up in the background.\n\nEven when I’m just sitting at my computer, or after dinner when I should be relaxing, my body feels like it’s bracing for something. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your worrying affects most areas of your life, such as work, family, and personal relationships, or is it more contained to just one or two areas?\n\nPatient: I think it’s more across different areas, not just one or two… though I don’t want to overstate it.\n\nMy mind kind of moves from one concern to the next—like my sister in Arecibo, then whether I missed something at work, then my husband driving home, church commitments, bills. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your worrying and tension would be better described as being about a wide range of things in your life, rather than focused on just one or a few specific issues?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think that’s probably right. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are your worries usually about a specific situation or topic, or are they more general and varied throughout the day?\n\nPatient: It’s more general, I think. I wish it were just one thing, but my mind kind of moves from one concern to another during the day. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a sudden feeling of intense fear or discomfort that reached a peak within minutes, even if it wasn't recently?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like a sudden wave that peaks really fast like that. I get worried and tense, yes, but it’s more of a buildup or it stays in the background. I can’t remember a time where it came on out of nowhere and hit a peak within minutes. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are your worries usually about a specific situation or topic, or are they more general and varied throughout the day?\n\nPatient: It’s more general, I think. I wish it were just one thing, but my mind kind of moves from one concern to another during the day. Like I’ll worry about my sister in Arecibo, then whether I missed something at the office, then my husband driving home, or church commitments, bills… things like that.\n\nAnd I know some of it is me getting ahead of myself, preparing for problems that haven’t even happened. I don’t mean to make it sound dramatic, but it does jump around like that. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Is there one particular thing that you worry about more than others?\n\nPatient: Not one single thing, no… or at least I don’t think so. It’s more like my mind keeps moving from one concern to the next. I’ll think about my sister in Arecibo, then whether I missed something at the office, then my husband driving home, church commitments, bills… it sort of rotates.\n\nI try to tell myself I’m just being prepared, but sometimes I’m preparing for problems that haven’t even happened. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a sudden feeling of intense fear or discomfort that reached a peak within minutes, even if it wasn't recently?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like a sudden wave that peaks really fast like that. I get worried and tense, yes, but it’s more of a buildup or it stays in the background. I can’t remember a time where it came on out of nowhere and hit a peak within minutes. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about your anxiety interfering with your overall well-being or quality of life?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… though I don’t want to make it sound like I’m falling apart or anything. I’m still getting my work done, and I show up for my family and church things. But I have noticed it takes me longer to finish reports because I keep double-checking them, more than I used to.\n\nAnd… I guess I’ve been a little short with people I care about sometimes, and then I feel bad and apologize. So yes, it does affect my quality of life some, even if I’m trying to manage it. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Is your worrying more focused on one area of your life, such as work or family, or is it spread across many different areas?\n\nPatient: It’s spread out, I think. I mean, work is part of it, and family too, but it doesn’t stay in one lane. My mind kind of jumps around—from my sister in Arecibo, to whether I missed something at the office, to my husband driving home, church things, bills… little things and bigger things.\n\nI try to frame it as being responsible or prepared, but sometimes I’m preparing for problems that haven’t even happened yet. I don’t know if that answers it the right way, but it’s not just one area.\n\nDoctor: Does your worrying or anxiety feel like it's always there in the background, even when you're not thinking about anything in particular?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s a fair way to put it. I don’t mean that I’m panicking all day or anything like that, but it does feel like I’m sort of wound up in the background.\n\nEven when I’m just sitting at my computer, or after dinner when I should be relaxing, my body feels like it’s bracing for something. Like I’m ready for a problem, even if I can’t point to one right then. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your worrying affects most areas of your life, such as work, family, and personal relationships, or is it more contained to just one or two areas?\n\nPatient: I think it’s more across different areas, not just one or two… though I don’t want to overstate it.\n\nMy mind kind of moves from one concern to the next—like my sister in Arecibo, then whether I missed something at work, then my husband driving home, church commitments, bills. It’s like I’m trying to be prepared, but sometimes I’m preparing for problems that haven’t even happened. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your worrying and tension would be better described as being about a wide range of things in your life, rather than focused on just one or a few specific issues?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think that’s probably right. It’s not just one specific thing I worry about.\n\nMy mind kind of keeps moving from one concern to another—my sister in Arecibo, then whether I missed something at the office, then my husband driving home, church commitments, bills… things like that. I tell myself I’m just trying to stay on top of things, but sometimes I realize I’m preparing for problems that haven’t even happened. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Is there a particular area in your life where you feel your worrying is most prominent?\n\nPatient: I guess if I had to pick, work is probably one place it shows up a lot… wondering if I missed something at the office or didn’t follow up on the right thing.\n\nBut it doesn’t really stay only there. My mind kind of jumps around—to my sister in Arecibo, my husband driving home, church commitments, bills. I’ll catch myself trying to prepare for problems that haven’t even happened yet. I don’t know if that’s the kind of thing you’re asking."
